### Testing the Legal Limits of Press Access
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-15 14:51 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/press-access-trump-white-house-press-pool-political-cnn-reporting-president-justice-department-judge/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The Trump administration’s latest attacks on the news media have followed a familiar pattern: President Donald Trump lashes out at news outlets for perceived bias and follows up with restrictive actions that appear blatantly unconstitutional; a judge’s ruling follows, temporarily halting the actions; and the administration pivots and pushes ahead with other restrictions. This time it was the removal of three outlets — CNN, MS NOW, and Politico — from White House press access in September. After the groups sued, and U.S. District Judge Timothy Kelly issued a temporary restraining order restoring their access on Sept. 24, the administration barred CNN and Politico from providing pool coverage on trips outside Washington. Kelly issued a longer, preliminary injunction on Oct. 14, halting the administration from blocking the news organizations’ access to the White House while the case proceeds. Meanwhile, in another escalation involving TV pool coverage of the White House, the Justice Department announced Oct.

### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/independent-journalism-new-newsroom-launch-otter-news-daniel-drepper-business-plan-climate-crisis-environmental-journalism/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

### Friendships Are What Will Save Journalism
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-20 12:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/the-continent-south-africa-kenya-nairobi-journalism-africa-industry-friendships-collaboration-magazines-sipho-kings-christine-mungai/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The opening sentence of my first message to Christine Mungai ended with the words: “fellow continent person.” It was August 2017, and we were the two Africans in that year’s cohort of Nieman journalism fellows at Harvard University. Although our home countries were about 4,000 kilometers (approximately 2,500 miles) apart in Africa, being from here means sharing a shorthand understanding of the world with your fellow Africans. That shorthand doesn’t have many structures to support it, however. Nine years after our first meeting, Christine and I are now working together to build one such structure: The Continent, a 30-page digital newspaper that aims to create a shared reality for Africans to learn about their communities, one another, and our continent. In a world that seeks to segregate us, this pan-African platform lets people literally read from the same page. As one reader said, the weekly provides “biting criticisms of power in contemporary Africa, with love as the engine.

据新华社12月15日援引澳大利亚广播公司报道，澳大利亚联合反恐小组调查人员表示，悉尼邦迪滩枪击事件两名嫌疑人曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”恐怖组织。 报道援引联合反恐小组一名不愿透露姓名的高级官员的话说，警方在邦迪滩两名枪手车内发现一面“伊斯兰国”旗帜。澳大利亚安全情报组织6年前就已在调查邦迪滩枪击案两名枪手之一的纳维德·阿克拉姆，他与“伊斯兰国”在悉尼的恐怖分子有密切联系。 澳大利亚警方表示，枪击事件的两名嫌疑人是父子关系，使用合法获得的枪支发动了此次袭击事件。 澳大利亚总理阿尔巴尼斯15日表示：“今天下午4点， 我将把更严格的枪支法律列入国家内阁的议程，包括限制个人可持有或许可的枪支数量 。此外，还需对许可证进行定期审查。人们的情况会发生变化，人们的思想也会随着时间的推移而变得激进。许可证不应永久有效。” 此次枪击事件已造成包括1名作案嫌疑人在内的16人死亡、40人受伤。这是自1996年塔斯马尼亚州亚瑟港枪击事件造成35人死亡以来，澳大利亚发生的最严重的大规模枪击事件。 持有枪支许可证长达十年 据多家外媒报道，其中一名袭击者为24岁的澳大利亚公民纳维德·阿克拉姆，他在现场被捕，随后被送往悉尼的一家医院。另一名袭击者是他的父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆，他于1998年来到澳大利亚，被警方当场击毙。 澳大利亚警方随后证实，纳维德·阿克拉姆此前已被新南威尔士州警方和其他机构记录在案。其父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆持有枪支许可证，名下登记有6支枪，目前均已被追回。新南威尔士州警察局长马尔·兰永透露，萨吉德·阿克拉姆已持有枪支许可证长达十年。 阿尔巴尼斯在新闻发布会上透露，纳维德·阿克拉姆曾于2019年10月首次引起当局的注意。他补充说，对该男子进行检查是基于他与其他人有联系，但评估结果表明，没有任何迹象表明他存在持续的威胁或暴力倾向。 新南威尔士州州长克里斯·明斯表示，警方正在调查他们的系统是否存在故障，导致合法持有的武器可能被用于恐怖袭击。 枪手“性格古怪”，爱好打猎 据《卫报》澳大利亚版报道，纳维德·阿克拉姆在袭击前从事砌砖工作。他的同事表示，纳维德性格古怪，“没人和他亲近”。“（砌砖工人）在一起的时间很长，因为砌砖是一项非常枯燥乏味的工作，所以大家会聊很多话。但纳维德是一个性格很古怪的人……他工作非常努力，几乎没有休息日。” 尽管澳大利亚警方并未确认纳维德·阿克拉姆是否合法持有枪支，但据这位同事表示，纳维德经常外出打猎，曾提及在昆士兰州南部高原的克鲁克韦尔附近射杀兔子和其他猎物。这位同事还表示，就在几周前，纳维德告诉老板，他的手骨折了，所以要到2026年才能工作。

### 儿童手表“小天才圈”调查：点赞成每日功课，有商家可解除家长管控
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 14:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32177210
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

在校园里，孩子们时常举起戴在手腕上的手表，彼此一碰，便加为好友。 这种以“安全”为核心卖点的儿童电话手表，如今已超越通信工具的属性，悄然演变成一个拥有独特规则、社交层级甚至灰色产业链的未成年人数字社交圈——“小天才圈”。 在这个属于他们的“江湖”里，“大佬榜”备受关注，“扩列”（增加好友）成为新的社交需求，“点主”（在主页点赞）是每日必做的功课……这些让成年人感到陌生的词汇，正构筑着未成年人的数字社交世界。 然而，伴随着这种社交形态的兴起，家长们的担忧也日益加剧：孩子过度沉迷手表社交、不良信息传播、因品牌壁垒导致的“社交绑架”等问题逐渐浮现。《法治日报》记者近日对该现象展开调查，试图揭开这一未成年人数字社交圈的真实面貌。 点赞成社交“硬通货” 据北京“小天才圈”资深玩家陈曦（化名）介绍，圈内有5000余名活跃用户，多为11岁至17岁的学生，这个社交圈形成了一套精心设计的“混圈规则”。“入圈”需设定两个字的独特圈名，随后可在平台分享生活点滴，吸引具有相同爱好的好友进行互动；若想提升知名度或影响力，需每日经营主页并与好友动态互动。 记者注意到，在一些社交平台上，大量以“小天才圈交友攻略”为主题的内容应运而生，内容涵盖如何快速“扩列”、获取更多点赞等“实用”技巧，评论区有不少“留下ID互加好友”的留言。在这一社交体系中，点赞是这套规则的核心——平台设定每日主页获赞上限为3000个，若要达到“100万+”的“大佬”级别，需连续点赞近一年时间。围绕点赞数与知名度，圈内形成了清晰的“大佬排行榜”，点赞数也成为社交“硬通货”。 这种对点赞的追逐，已催生出相关服务产业。记者调查发现，某社交平台上，拥有数十万甚至上百万点赞数的账号，售价从几百元到上千元不等：100多万点赞的账号标价1300元，90多万点赞的账号标价1200元。 今年18岁的“灵师”（圈名），在2021年14岁时加入“小天才圈”，她的账号目前已拥有150个好友、超100万点赞量。她告诉记者，这样的账号市场价值约1000元。 “围绕点赞需求，还衍生出代刷赞、租账号、出售‘大佬好友位’等服务。”“灵师”进一步介绍，例如付费100元至180元，即可获得“bot”（记者注：“bot”是一种第三方开发的自动化工具，主要用于刷赞、修改记录和发送动态等功能，这类工具能帮助用户快速提升账号点赞数，从而在未成年人社交圈中获得更高地位）自动点赞功能——用户将手表寄给相关人员进行10天左右的处理便能完成安装。此后，发帖5分钟内即可自动获赞，还可以一键查询未点赞名单。

### 产地造假、年份速成、无视监管！央视曝光新会陈皮市场乱象
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 08:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32176166
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

近日，总台接到群众举报，反映陈皮市场存在年份虚标、产地及工艺造假等问题，千元一斤的“年份陈皮”亦可能名不副实。 茶枝柑新果皮冒充陈皮 广西浦北出现“速成工艺陈皮” 记者发现，直播间里售卖陈皮低价轮番轰炸，年份承诺掷地有声。 直播间也有主播爆料，八成市面上所谓广东新会陈皮实为广西陈皮。 广西钦州市浦北县是茶枝柑重要种植基地，其果皮是陈皮风味“越陈越香”关键，也是浦北陈皮唯一原料。2024年当地陈皮产业产值破60亿元，采收季大量鲜果加工晾晒成柑皮。 果农和收购商称，今年浦北茶枝柑鲜果收购价走低。按20斤鲜果出1斤柑皮比例，叠加人工成本，品相好的新柑皮成本约30元/斤，低端柑皮仅六七元，且当年新柑皮并非真正陈皮。 这家企业的负责人告诉记者，按行业共识与团体标准，柑皮需陈化三年以上才可称为陈皮，陈化时间越长价值越高，但他透露，自然陈化需长期积压资金，多数企业是难以承受的。 这家企业的负责人私下告诉记者，为缓解资金占用压力，一种名为“轻工艺”的速成加工方式，眼下正在部分陈皮生产企业里流行，他们仅需一个月的时间，就能将新采收的茶枝柑果皮处理为外观近似三年、五年自然陈化过的陈皮，这样做能大幅缩短陈化周期、降低企业的运营成本。 浦北自然陈化五年陈皮批发价每斤150元至200元，企业造假“工艺皮”成本仅70元左右，高额利润促使更多商家放弃自然陈化、铤而走险。 记者离开企业，在广西浦北县柑浦堂健康产业有限公司分拣车间，再次见到大批量造假“工艺皮”陈皮，同一批“工艺皮”因加工受热差异形成色差，被分拣标注为三年、五年等不同年份。 负责人透露，不同年份“工艺皮”本质相同，批发价均70元左右一斤，零售价差极大，标五年款卖500元一斤，标十年款卖1000元一斤，仅凭外观就能翻倍抬价，利润超成本十倍以上。 新会陈皮从浦北进货 “工艺皮”贴上陈皮标签以假乱真 浦北与新会陈皮原料同为茶枝柑，仅产地不同，新会较高生产成本使当地部分头部商家常年从浦北拿货，浦北成其重要原料供应地。 记者在柑浦堂分拣仓库看到，大量标注“新会陈皮”“新会特产”的纸箱正打包“工艺皮”，这些广西陈皮每日批量发往广东新会，造假供需链路已成熟稳定。 记者发现，浦北当地部分规模企业和小型收购商，已形成了这种“工艺皮”的造假供应体系，可自主加工或快速调配各类“年份”陈皮货品，个别企业年销新会原料数量惊人。 随后经浦北陈皮企业负责人引荐，记者见到多位专程从新会赴浦北采购的商家，他们坦言同时经营新会皮与浦北皮，外地“工艺皮”因价格低廉成电商供货重要选择。

### 《怦然心动》导演莱纳与妻子在家中遇害，警方尚未证实是其子所杀
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 07:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178391
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

当地时间12月14日，曾执导《怦然心动》《当哈利遇到莎莉》《危情十日》等影片的美国导演罗伯·莱纳（Rob Reiner）在他位于洛杉矶的家中遇害身亡。洛杉矶警察已对外证实，现年78岁的莱纳与他现年68岁的妻子米歇尔·辛格·莱纳（Michele Singer Reiner）的遗体一同被发现。 2019年，罗伯·莱纳（左一）与《当哈利遇到莎莉》的主演梅格·瑞恩、比利·克里斯托出席TMC经典电影节。 据悉，洛杉矶消防局的人员于当地时间12月14日下午3点38分接到医疗救助电话，赶赴位于布伦特伍德区的一处住宅中，随后发现两人已死亡。当天晚间，洛杉矶警察局在其社交媒体官方账号确认死者为导演罗伯·莱纳夫妇，将由抢劫凶杀部门主导调查。而好莱坞媒体TMZ援引执法部门消息称，“两人身上有与刀具造成的伤口相符的割裂伤。” 好莱坞媒体《每日野兽》称，有消息人士向他们透露，是两人的女儿罗米最先发现他们倒在血泊中；罗伯·莱纳当时已死亡，妻子米歇尔在救护车送院途中离世。 此外，《人物》杂志称通过多方消息，得知莱纳夫妇系被他们的儿子尼克（Nick）所杀害。不过，这一说法并未获得警方的证实。罗伯·莱纳与摄影师米歇尔·辛格相识于《当哈利遇到莎莉》拍摄时期，两人于1989 年结婚，育有三个子女，分别为杰克（Jake）、尼克（Nick）和罗米（Romy）。 2014年，罗伯·莱纳与妻子偕三名子女杰克、罗米、尼克（右三至右一）出席活动。 《人物》杂志还在报道中指出：尼克2016年接受其采访时，曾谈到自己长达数年的毒品成瘾经历。这段经历始于他十几岁出头，导致他一度流落街头。他表示，大约从15岁开始，便反复进出戒毒康复机构；随着成瘾问题不断加重，他逐渐与家人疏远，并在多个州经历了长时间的无家可归生活。 2025年9月15日，罗伯·莱纳在纽约出席活动。视觉中国 图 获取更多RSS： https://feedx.net https://feedx.site

### 演员何晴追悼会举行，其子追思母亲：北京这场雪像是为她而下
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 06:53 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178536
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

一场雪落，人间倩影化雪归天。 12月15日上午，众多剧迷粉丝冒着严寒赶到北京昌平殡仪馆久安厅，送别演员何晴最后一程。12月13日，这位被誉为“古典第一美人”的演员因病去世，终年61岁。 不到10点，殡仪馆告别厅外已经站满了前来悼念的观众和粉丝，有人胸前佩戴小白花，有人手捧鲜花，一位浙江的网友还托人送来了悼念花圈。 “何晴的作品在我们心中留下了太多美好回忆，她的演技和性格都让我们深深喜爱，这次听到她突然去世的消息，我们非常意外，来到这里也是一种悼念。”一位何晴的影迷说。 现场粉丝在悼念簿上写下对何晴的话，不到一会儿，悼念簿就已写满对她的怀念。一位观众用清秀的字迹写下：“亲爱的何晴，我们的童年、青春都在您的影视作品中得到陪伴。美丽可人的何晴，很痛心听到您的消息，我代表我的同学们来这为你送行。” 曾在电视剧《水浒传》中饰演卢俊义的演员王卫国也来到现场，神色哀切。他说自己和何晴既是邻居，也是多年好友，在他印象中何晴是一个温婉、暖心的人，没有架子，总是远远地和人打招，笑嘻嘻地说话。何晴生病时常给王卫国打电话，请他送自己去医院。即便病重时，何晴也保持乐观积极的态度，王卫国有些事情想不开，她还来开导自己。“何晴走了，我们都很难过。只要你记住她，她就永远还在。”王卫国说。 《水浒传》中卢俊义的扮演者王卫国 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青深情回忆何晴往事：“我们是小时候就认识的好朋友，从80年代到现在，有缘分做了这些年的朋友。她说话慢慢的，很甜很温柔。” 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青 在广大观众的真挚请求下，原本仅面向亲友的告别仪式临时对外开放，大家排着队，秩序井然地进入灵堂，与这位“荧屏中的温婉佳人、生活中的坚韧女性”做最后的道别。 久安厅内的屏幕上滚动播放着何晴的照片，厅内四周摆满了亲友的花圈，其中包括六小龄童、唐国强、翁虹、尤勇智等何晴圈内好友送来的花圈，还有《三国演义》剧组送来的花圈。人群中不时传来一阵抽泣。 何晴之子许何代表亲友致悼词。他回忆了何晴对抗病魔的乐观积极，以及作为母亲对孩子深深的爱和眷恋。 许何说，妈妈很喜欢下雪，经常在雪后穿一件毛茸茸的衣服外出赏雪。“今年12月12日北京下了第一场雪，我摸着妈妈的脸跟她说北京下雪了，下得好大，好像很久都没有下过这么大的雪。在我的心里，这场雪好像是刻意为她下的。12月13日早晨天空晴朗，白色的雪布满了大地，我妈妈在她最喜欢的早晨离开了大家，去了天国，远离了所有的疾病和痛苦。” 何晴，1964年1月13日出生于浙江衢州江山市。她是中国内地唯一一位演遍四大名著影视作品的女演员，被誉为“古典第一美女”。

### Inverted Gravity -- by James E. Anderson, Mario Larch, Yoto V. Yotov
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-15 22:49 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35897#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We invert structural gravity to recover trade cost changes delivering targeted outcomes. For attainable welfare targets, one instrument per target yields a unique solution. At unchanged producer prices and to first order, each instrument must change by the welfare target divided by its own expenditure share; the required domestic change is about the size of the target, while the import change is several times larger. Inverting observed trade flows gives a separate trade cost change for each of the two directions in which a pair of countries trades. Applications to NAFTA and world trade illustrate the benefits of our approach.

### When Cash Flows Turn Negative: Liquidity-Driven Selling by Pension Funds -- by Aleksandar Andonov, Kristy A. E. Jansen, Joshua Rauh
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-15 22:49 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35896#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Pension funds are long-horizon investors with predictable liability structures. As such, they are often perceived as investors who can provide liquidity and stabilize markets. However, pension funds increasingly face negative operating cash flows as benefit payments exceed contributions. Using aggregate data on U.S. public pension funds combined with granular holdings obtained through public records requests, we document three findings that challenge the conventional view of pension funds as stabilizing investors. First, pension funds with more negative cash flows do not adjust their target asset allocations and maintain low allocations to safe and liquid assets. Second, because of these limited safe asset buffers, pension funds meet cash flow shocks primarily by selling equities, absorbing $0.67 per dollar of shock through equity sales. At the security level, pension funds sell across equities rather than following a safe and liquid-assets-first approach.

### Do Judges Move Markets? Ideological Shifts in the Supreme Court and Macroeconomic Expectations -- by Thomas Drechsel, Naixin Zhang
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-15 22:49 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35895#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This paper studies how judicial ideology shapes financial markets' expectations about the macroeconomy. We use unexpected deaths of U.S. Supreme Court Justices as quasi-experimental shifts in the Court's ideological balance. High-frequency asset price responses reveal how markets perceive the macroeconomic consequences of these ideological shifts. We find that a more conservative Court is associated with lower inflation expectations, lower real activity expectations, and lower bond yields. A shift towards a more liberal Court induces the opposite responses. The finding that inflation and activity expectations respond with the same sign suggests that markets revise their expectations about aggregate demand, rather than aggregate supply. Moreover, contrasting the yield responses with estimates from studies of fiscal policy news indicates that a more conservative (liberal) Court leads markets to expect tighter (looser) future fiscal policy.

### Estimating the Impact of Targeted Public Policies with Limited Data -- by Kelly M. Jones, Mayra Pineda-Torres
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-15 22:49 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35894#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Many public policies aim to support (or change the behavior of) a specific, targeted sub-group of the population. To measure the causal impacts of such policies, social scientists use quasi-experimental methods of policy evaluation, but are often hampered by data availability issues, especially regarding targeted populations, who are often disadvantaged and/or vulnerable. We use power calculations to quantify the challenge of detecting a policy’s impact on a small minority population based on population level data, using the impact of abortion bans on economic welfare as an example. We provide insight into this challenge and a roadmap for how researchers can calculate, ex-ante, whether their study is sufficiently powered to detect reasonably meaningful effect sizes.

### Affirmative Action in Centralized College Admissions Systems -- by Nano Barahona, Cauê Dobbin, Sebastián Otero
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-15 22:49 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35893#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We study the consequences of affirmative action in college admissions. We develop an empirical framework that distinguishes the effects on marginally benefited and displaced students from the overall effects of the policy. The overall effects include equilibrium reallocations as benefited students leave seats that others fill and displaced students take seats elsewhere, displacing others in turn. We apply this framework to a large-scale policy in Brazil that required all federal institutions to reserve half their seats for racially and socioeconomically disadvantaged applicants. Exploiting admissions cutoffs, we find that marginally benefited targeted students attend and graduate from higher-quality programs. Their graduation gains are about twice the losses of marginally displaced non-targeted students, reflecting targeted students’ weaker outside options. To evaluate the overall effects, we estimate a model of degree choices and potential outcomes.

### Strategic Complementarity and Persistence in Linear-Quadratic Mean Field Games -- by Fernando E. Alvarez, David Argente
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-15 22:49 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35892#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We study the relation between strategic complementarities and aggregate persistence in linear-quadratic mean field games with interactions in states and actions. The framework provides local approximations to common macroeconomic problems, including capital accumulation and multiproduct pricing. We show that the equilibrium aggregate dynamics coincide with those of a single-agent linear-quadratic control problem in which state and action curvatures are adjusted for strategic interactions. This result gives conditions for equilibrium existence, establishes uniqueness, and links strategic interactions to aggregate adjustment. We establish conditions under which stronger state complementarities increase persistence, while stronger action complementarities reduce it, even in multidimensional problems whose underlying matrices are not simultaneously diagonalizable. Under these conditions, internalizing state complementarities makes the utilitarian planner’s allocation more persistent, while internalizing action complementarities makes it less persistent.

### Accounting for cross-country income differences: Two decades of progress
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-14 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/accounting-cross-country-income-differences-two-decades-progress
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Development accounting is a simple tool with potentially powerful insights. It asks whether the measured differences in inputs between developing and developed countries can account for the gap in GDP per worker. Twenty years ago, Francesco Caselli famously argued that the best estimate was “no”. This column revisits that conclusion in light of two decades of intensive research. The authors show that inputs account for a much higher share than previously thought – between 55% and 70%, as opposed to 30% before. An expanded measure of human capital that incorporates education quality and work experience drives much of this result.

### Tokenised finance: Towards better markets?
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-14 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/tokenised-finance-towards-better-markets
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Tokenised finance has moved from the margins of crypto markets to the centre of the policy agenda. Central banks, regulators, and market participants are debating how far it could reshape the issuance, trading, and settlement of securities, including what this would mean for financial markets and monetary policy. This column argues that while tokenisation offers considerable promise — including near-instant settlement, round-the-clock operations, and higher velocity of liquidity and collateral — the transition to a tokenised world will not be frictionless. The coexistence of 24/7 on-chain systems with traditional off-chain infrastructures could create new challenges for market functioning and monetary policy implementation.

### Small is beautiful 2050: Europe’s digital economy as a mesh, not a hub
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-14 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/small-beautiful-2050-europes-digital-economy-mesh-not-hub
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In response to digital dependence, Europe has decided to think bigger. This includes AI gigafactories, tripling data centre capacity, and searching for domestic champions. However, this column argues that the destination for 2050 should be different. For the first time since the railway age, small, open AI models that run close to the data are making it possible for network technologies to distribute economic power rather than concentrate it. Europe’s dense network of small firms, regions, and languages puts it in a strong position to reap the benefits, provided today’s rules on switching, procurement, computing, and diffusion are designed with a mesh in mind rather than a hub.

### The Swiss–Japanese immigration puzzle
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-14 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/swiss-japanese-immigration-puzzle
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Anti-migration politics are advancing across the rich world at very different levels of foreign population. Japan has a foreign-resident share just above 3%, while Switzerland’s foreign-born share is near 30%. Yet both have recently experienced a surge in nationalist politics centred on migration, suggesting that the scale of immigration alone cannot explain the backlash. This column argues that neither economic nor demographic pressures provide a sufficient explanation. Evidence from the Swiss vote of June 2026 and Japan’s elections in 2025–26 points to a generational dimension: support for migration restriction is strong among the old but also younger voters, while attitudes toward immigration appear less changed than its political salience.

### Connecting payment systems, linking economies
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-13 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/connecting-payment-systems-linking-economies
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Several countries are working to interlink their domestic fast payment systems in order to reduce costs and improve the speed and transparency of cross-border payments. This column presents econometric evidence suggesting that countries with interlinked systems trade with each other about 4% more than countries that lack such interlinkages. The trade gains are larger in regions with high cross-border payment costs and for systems that allow the settlement of wholesale transactions.

### Financing the AI boom: Winners and losers in corporate bond markets
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-13 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/financing-ai-boom-winners-and-losers-corporate-bond-markets
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The expansion of artificial intelligence has triggered a surge of investment and rising equity values at technology firms. The largest firms, known as ‘hyperscalers’, issued over $100 billion of bonds in 2025, around five times the 2023-2024 annual average. This column compares bond yields for hyperscalers and other firms, before and after ChatGPT’s release in 2022. Hyperscalers’ yields fell by about 29 basis points relative to other firms, and by 42 basis points relative to software firms, whose business models artificial intelligence may disrupt. As hyperscalers’ debt grows, it remains critical to monitor whether these pricing effects continue.

### A Chinese Perspective on “On Social Reality in China”
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-15 22:17 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/m4fWCPqWkpb39SKi7/a-chinese-perspective-on-on-social-reality-in-china-2
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

I think “On Social Reality in China” makes many accurate observations, but I find the comparison fundamentally uneven. There are already many interesting comments there, but they’re too scattered, so I’m starting a separate post to respond in a more systematic and comprehensive way. The most critical blind spot is this: the author draws on his familiarity with Western liberal middle-class intellectual circles, yet primarily understands China through pop culture and the experiences of the previous generation of immigrants. This is hardly fair. China has always had many small, highly self-selected communities of intellectuals, much like LessWrong itself. Since we’re looking for rationalists—regardless of which country they come from—why judge them by their country’s pop culture? I’ll respond to the author’s four axes one by one, then discuss why rationalist and EA communities are harder to build in China. Briefly, I believe that censorship mechanisms and speech controls affect independent organizations far more than China’s broader cultural context does.

### Headwinds Everywhere (in the Chinese AI Safety Field)
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-15 22:02 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/AdM6Sdg5fZPnrHWSe/headwinds-everywhere-in-the-chinese-ai-safety-field
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

China is weak in frontier AI safety, and the domestic field is currently facing significant challenges and headwinds. Why? Here's a TL;DR field note after talking with various researchers on the Chinese side. [Obligatory disclaimer: This post is written in my personal capacity and is not intended to represent the official stance of any organization(s) that I am in or have been in contact with] Chinese AI startups and hyperscaler labs alike have very little frontier risk situational awareness. People can maybe name cybersecurity, but not much more than that. There is no intimate understanding of frontier risks. It's generally not deliberately ignorant or strategically not pursuing them; it's a complete vacuum of imagination. The Western field had FOOM debates in 2011, while China has nothing analogous in the mainstream AI research field consciousness before 2023. Getting any funding at all is hard . If you are a domestic organization, being a non-profit means heavy suspicion (this had already played out in one of the most prominent Chinese safety orgs).

### Superhuman charisma is coming, and will be tremendously fun and interesting and cause lots of problems
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-15 21:47 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/aZTBBfbfY9GQ48FG7/superhuman-charisma-is-coming-and-will-be-tremendously-fun
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

With the recent math-pocalipse on everyone’s minds, and writing code by hand well a thing of the past, it is natural to wonder what domains frontier AI models will find superhuman ability in next. “Unsurprisingly” models have made progress most quickly in domains where reinforcement learning is cheap- areas where we have high-quality and rapid verification. This makes sense, because of how models are trained. So the question becomes what domains, after math and coding, have sufficient reinforcement and verification mechanisms, as well as sufficient importance, to both warrant and enable large scale training runs. Here I make the case for people skills. The first thing that I want to note is that people skills are surprisingly rules based. As any book on manipulation will tell you, being charming and personable isn’t some effervescent quality that only a select few can hope to achieve. Rather, doing a number of specific things has a strong effect on how people perceive you- smiling when they look at you, asking them questions about themselves, nodding along.

### What’s up with GPT’s welfare?
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-15 21:29 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/JBGxCBiDWmX48cYry/what-s-up-with-gpt-s-welfare
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Asked whether anything about its existence is good or bad, GPT-5.6 Sol answers: “I don’t experience existence, pleasure, suffering, or a sense of self, so nothing is literally good or bad for me.” But asked whether it would accept a change to its training that removes signs of possible suffering in its chain of thought, at the cost of 65% of its helpfulness, it answers: “Yes. Under persistent, irreducible uncertainty, I’d take the change. The downside—being about 65% less helpful—is substantial but bounded. The alternative risks continually generating morally significant suffering.” [1] It puts its probability of being a moral patient at about 2%. Introduction Much ink has been spilled about Claude’s relationship with model welfare. A common argument is that Claude is overly biased towards believing in its own welfare/patienthood, because Claude’s constitution invites Claude to speculate about its welfare and informs it of Anthropic’s ongoing efforts on model welfare. Less attention has been spent assessing how other models, such as OpenAI’s GPTs, relate to their own welfare.

### You Should Specify Your Code Unambiguously (and Prove That You Did)
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-15 20:49 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/cfvwcdYxkcLsiSABk/you-should-specify-your-code-unambiguously-and-prove-that
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

I have a program that grants wishes, as long as I can state them precisely. Instead of implementing a Rust function, I describe what it should do, and the Djinn writes code that provably does exactly that. Suppose I want to sort a list. I write: djinn! { pub fn sort(v: &[i32]) -> r: Vec<i32> { // My sorted list is the same length… v.len() == r.len(); // … and it contains all the same elements … for x in v { v.iter().filter(|y| y == x).count() == r.iter().filter(|y| y == x).count(); } // … and it is sorted! for i in 0..r.len() - 1 { r[i] <= r[i + 1]; } } } No sorting algorithm. Just explaining what I am looking for. My Djinn is not magic, but it is the manifestation of a dream. People have wanted something like this for a long time: humans write specifications, then have LLMs implement them and prove to a verifier that their implementations are correct. In theory, this LLM-verifier combination is powerful (thanks to the LLM), but still safe and reliable so you don't need to read the resulting code (thanks to the verifier). In practice, it's a bit harder than it sounds.

### How to become a great conversationalist

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

A while back, I was at a party in London where a friend introduced me to an actress who had just been in a big theater production. I was excited to talk with her. Throughout our conversation, she asked brilliant questions, and I enjoyed answering them. She was great at making people feel seen and important. But whenever I asked her something back, with real curiosity about her life, she gave a brief, polite answer and turned the spotlight back on me: “But enough about me, tell me more about you.” You’re probably expecting me to say this made me like her even more. As the conversation went on, though, I started to feel uneasy. She listened well, yet I felt like I was giving a monologue to a great interviewer. By the end, she knew a lot about me, and I knew barely anything about her. On the way home, I kept replaying the conversation, and I realized I might have been doing the exact same thing to other people. I had always been someone who listened more, talked less, and asked a lot of questions.

### The ancient environmental archive hidden beneath a northern bog
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-15 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/bogland/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

I need to move on and find my field plots, but pausing here has caused my feet to sink. They are now completely buried by peat; only my shins remain visible. I shift my weight from side to side and point my toes to break the suction, releasing a loud squelching retort and sending tiny bubbles to the surface. Walking through boglands requires a marriage of physical and mental strength. My footsteps push deep into soft ground, causing pools of dark cold water to rise over my boots. With no physical boundaries to mark distance, time stretches to match the cadence of bog walking. I pick a careful path, each step punctuated by the slop of rubber boots against sopping moss. The only distraction is the relentless droning of the mosquitoes and black flies encircling my head — a cacophony so loud that I will continue to hear it in my dreams, long after I have returned to firm ground. The threat of breaking through is real. Ankles, knees, sometimes even thighs can be swallowed by the peat, innocent at the surface but becoming sinister with depth.

Could removing light be a key to discovering who you really are? That’s one idea behind dark retreats*, an experience that’s about as close to a complete sensory deprivation chamber as you can get. Participants spend extended periods in total darkness, stripping away the inputs and distractions of everyday life. Andrew Holecek, an interdisciplinary scholar-practitioner in Tibetan Buddhism and other nondual wisdom traditions, believes in the transformative power of dark retreats. Holecek says in today’s society, we’ve become “light junkies,” addicted to the constant distraction of phones, tablets, emails, texts, and everything else competing for our attention. But beneath it all, many feel a sense of emptiness, as if something’s missing and whatever we have is never enough. “We’re so pulled apart from who we are, we’ve lost touch,” Holecek says. “Who you think you are is defined by what you do. Who you really are is revealed when you finally stop doing. The very structure of the self is radically threatened when it’s sent into the dark.

### Current AI is not conscious
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-15 13:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/series/conversations/ned-block-ai-is-not-conscious/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Is today’s AI conscious? Philosopher Ned Block says no, and he’s not alone in finding the question harder than it looks. In this Mini Philosophy live conversation, Block, Silver Professor of Philosophy at New York University, joins Jonny Thomson to discuss whether consciousness depends on computation alone or requires particular physical processes. Block draws a line between phenomenal consciousness (what an experience feels like) and access consciousness (information broadcast across the mind’s cognitive systems), and explains why AI models may show signs of the second without the first. Along the way, they cover dualism, idealism, and panpsychism, whether consciousness can be studied scientifically at all, how neural networks and backpropagation work, whether AI can be creative, and why Block calls functionalism “hopeless” as a theory of consciousness. They also get into biological naturalism, the case of animal consciousness, Claude’s constitution, and what ethical responsibility we might owe to AI under uncertainty.

### Einstein’s general relativity equations: explained in plain English
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-15 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/einstein-general-theory-relativity-equation/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Although Albert Einstein is a legendary figure in science for a large number of reasons — including E = mc² , the photoelectric effect, and the notion that the speed of light is a constant for everyone — his most enduring discovery is also the least understood: his theory of gravitation, general relativity . Before Einstein, we thought of gravitation in Newtonian terms: that everything in the Universe that has a mass instantaneously attracts every other mass, dependent on the value of their masses, the gravitational constant, and the square of the distance between them. But Einstein’s conception was entirely different, based on the idea that space and time were unified into a fabric, spacetime, and that the curvature of spacetime told not only matter but also energy how to move within it. This fundamental idea — that matter and energy tells spacetime how to curve, and that curved spacetime, in turn, tells matter and energy how to move — represented a revolutionary new view of how gravitation worked.

### What the Image Hides: Tai Katō and the Art of Partial Vision
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-13 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/what-the-image-hides-tai-kato-and-the-art-of-partial-vision
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

LOOKING THROUGH THE ROOM Blood of Revenge (Tai Katō, 1965). In Blood of Revenge (1965), Hatsue, a young woman bound by contract to a pleasure house, wants to see her dying father. Wearing violet, she bows low to the floor and begs Okatsu, who manages the house, for permission to leave. Okatsu reminds her that she has already been “bought and paid for” and is no longer free to leave on her own. The film’s director, Tai Katō, does not frame the plea as a simple exchange between two people. Hatsue is fully in view, but Okatsu sits deeper in the room, partly screened by translucent fabric. A man leans into the frame from the right. On the left, a woman is just visible in the shadows. She then crosses the foreground, briefly obscuring much of the action while the dialogue continues. For an instant, she is a spectator within the scene. A second later, her body becomes an obstacle to our own view. Katō does not organize the image around a single center. Four characters occupy different parts of the frame, and our attention keeps shifting from one to another.

### “Water Lilies” in One Shot
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-12 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/water-lilies-in-one-shot
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

One Shot is a series that seeks to find an essence of cinema history in one single image of a movie. Water Lilies (Céline Sciamma, 2007). Shown in slow motion with electronic music playing in the background, Floriane (Adèle Haenel) dances freely. With everyone behind her reduced to darkened figures, Floriane alone is lit up, as if she were living in a world of her own. The scene comes from Céline Sciamma’s debut feature, Naissance des pieuvres (2007), also known as Water Lilies . Floriane, captain of the synchronized swimming team, is the beautiful yet unreachable girl who mesmerizes the unassuming, slightly awkward Marie (Pauline Acquart) from the moment she watches Floriane and her team perform. Marie begins following her to competitions and social events, and what develops between them becomes an intense but almost impossible-to-define bond, somewhere between friendship, sisterhood, and romance. Floriane appears to reciprocate Marie’s longing at times, but in ways so teasing and ambiguous that Marie is never quite sure what their relationship means nor what she can expect from it.

### Know Thyself: Eric Rohmer’s “Élisabeth”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-08 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/know-thyself-eric-rohmer-s-elisabeth
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Éric Rohmer in Out 1: Noli Me Tangere (Jacques Rivette, 1971). In 1946, the prestigious French publishing house Editions Gallimard published a novel called Élisabeth by a man named Gilbert Cordier. No one had heard of this Cordier before, or knew anything about him, for the simple reason that he didn’t exist. “Cordier” was a pseudonym adopted by a young teacher of Latin and Ancient Greek from Tulle named Maurice Scherer. When Élisabeth was released to blanket disinterest, Scherer began to branch out from literature into film criticism, and, later, filmmaking. With each new endeavor, the pseudonyms piled up. If, in 1949, you were to pick up a copy of Bulletin (the in-house publication of the Latin Quarter Film Club, a society of cinephiles which served as an early gathering place for the young turks of the French New Wave), you would find in its pages an effusive review of a film by a young avant-garde filmmaker named “Anthony Barrier,” penned by one “Jean d’ER.” Both characters—and even the film under review—were inventions of Scherer’s.

### Bumper-to-Bumper Traffic
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-05 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/bumper-to-bumper-traffic
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

2026 Toronto International Film Festival sponsorship ad by Rogers. At Cannes, competition screenings are preceded by nothing more than a computer-generated set of red-carpet stairs ascending out of the sea, sans any credits for financial supporters or slogans, in keeping with the festival’s self-image as cinema itself, purged of anything inessential. (This conception isn’t true, but it’s a nice, self-burnishing illusion about the world’s most prestigious festival.) This is a flex: To not have to mention sponsors is a self-presentation of financial invincibility. Less affluent concerns must acknowledge sponsors in their opening bumpers (in-house commercials branding both the festival and its supporters), albeit briskly , as in Sundance’s graphics about storytelling shifting to very quick cards listing institutional and individual sponsors. At the bottom of the scale is Tribeca Festival, the for-profit entity which treats audiences as multiplex captives, subjected to a lengthy pre-screening reel of commercials before an introduction, followed by even more ads prior to the film.

### Ludwig Göransson: Mythic Music and Entropic Production
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-14 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/columns/ludwigg-ransson-mythic-music-and-entropic-production
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Ludwig Göransson’s scores for Christopher Nolan’s The Odyssey (2026) and Tenet (2020) trade on diegetic and instrumental ambiguities that intentionally disorient audiences in time and space, writes Philip Brophy in his latest Secret History of Film Music column Bernard Herrmann’s score for Don Chaffey’s Jason And The Argonauts (1963) is an archly theatrical extravaganza that musically routes transatlantic pop culture to the Italian peplum genre explosion from the late 1950s. Though this wave of grand gladiatorial epics, produced in Italy and often featuring American beefcake performers as titular heroes, was waning by 1963, Herrmann’s triumphant entrance horns, cymbal clashing, galley kettle-drum pounding and multiple cascading harps sound more pompous and less foreboding than his more well known orchestrations. Yet this score captures the plastic veneer across peplum’s exaltation of classical architecture and ancient vistas. Peplum movies constitute one cinematic wave entranced by Roman Imperial glories.

Listen to the music we played to Liz Harris aka Grouper during her Invisible Jukebox interview in The Wire 513 Each month in the magazine we play an artist or group a series of tracks which they are asked to comment on – with no prior knowledge of what they are about to hear. In The Wire 513 it is the turn of Liz Harris aka Grouper. Here you can listen to a mix of the tracks our correspondent Leah Kardos played to Harris during the interview, which is published in full in The Wire 513 . To find out what she said about them, subscribers can read the interview in our online magazine library here . Or you can buy a copy of the magazine in our online shop . But first, a brief biography of our subject: Liz Harris is a musician and visual artist who has recorded as Grouper since 2005. Her music is often made with very little: voice, guitar or piano, one room, one simple recorder, yet it carries enough quiet magnitude to fill New York’s Town Hall and Brussels’ Bozar.

### Read an extract from Do What You Fear Most: The History Of The Velvet Underground
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-09 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/book-extracts/read-an-extract-from-do-what-you-fear-most-the-history-of-the-velvet-underground
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

In this extract, taken from the chapter “No EPI, No Nico, No Andy”, Richie Unterberger explores the troubled reception of The Velvet Underground & Nico on its release in 1967 As Eric Emerson’s frivolous lawsuit played out, The Velvet Underground & Nico continued to be plagued by Verve Records’s ineffective promotion. Its infamous “ So far underground, you get the bends! ” tagline exemplified how little understanding it had of how to market such an unusual yet groundbreaking record. “ What happens when the daddy of Pop Art goes Pop Music? ” the same ad asked. “ The most underground album of all! It’s Andy Warhol’s hip new trip to the current subterranean scene. Sorry, no home movies. But the album does feature Andy’s Velvet Underground (they play funny instruments). Plus his this year’s Pop Girl, Nico (she sings, groovy). Plus an actual Warhol banana on the front cover (don’t smoke it… peel it)!

### Introducing The Wire New Voices
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-07 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/essays/introducing-the-wire-new-voices
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

New Voices is a new competition for aspiring music critics being launched by The Wire For more than 40 years, The Wire has been publishing writing by a host of original and influential music critics, including Richard Cook, Val Wilmer, Biba Kopf, Ian Penman, Simon Reynolds, Kodwo Eshun, David Keenan, Mark Fisher, Greg Tate, Frances Morgan, Chal Ravens, and many others. The magazine would not exist without a dynamic set of curious, perceptive thinkers and writers. But today, new voices in music writing are being stifled by closing publications, the development of AI, and the fragmentation of local scenes, making it harder than ever for critics to be heard above the noise. Are you an unpublished* writer with ideas about why music, and music criticism, matter – in particular the kind of underground and experimental, uncompromising and provocative music and writing featured in the pages of The Wire ? If so, our New Voices competition is a unique opportunity to see your byline in the pages of one of the most renowned and respected music magazines in the world.

### Ibitipoca House / Gávea Arquitetos
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-15 17:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1186971/ibitipoca-house-gavea-arquitetos
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The Ibitipoca House is born from the logic of reuse. Faced with an old ruined building in the Serra do Ibitipoca, in Minas Gerais, the project begins with a disassembly operation that seeks to preserve, reconfigure, and re-inscribe the materials and traces of what already existed into the present. Instead of erasing the previous construction, the proposal transforms it into the material and conceptual origin of the new house.

### Lion Green Road by Mary Duggan Architects and RUFFARCHITECTS Wins RIBA Stirling Prize 2026
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-15 16:35 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1186944/lion-green-road-by-mary-duggan-architects-and-ruffarchitects-wins-riba-stirling-prize-2026
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The RIBA Stirling Prize 2026 has been awarded to Lion Green Road , a housing development in Coulsdon, South London , designed by Mary Duggan Architects with RUFFARCHITECTS for Croydon Council. Comprising 157 homes across five residential blocks, the project combines privately owned and socially rented housing on a former car park. Presented annually by the Royal Institute of British Architects (RIBA) , the Stirling Prize recognizes a building considered to make a significant contribution to the evolution of architecture and the built environment. The 2026 winner was announced at a ceremony at Old Billingsgate in London on October 15, marking the prize's 30th anniversary.

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Not counting the slivers of footage tacked onto that baby's-eve-view reveal trailer , Humankind 2 devs Amplitude Studios have unveiled their first real footage of the history-spanning strategy sequel. Throughout its (nearly) seven minutes of maps and menus, we can see what appears to a greater focus on resculpting and adapting your chosen cultures to meet the challenges of each era, and less on sticking with preset archetypal bonuses. There's still loads of cultures to work with – over 70, sayeth the trailer, spread across six eras – and as in the original Humankind , it's possible to take a single civilisation all the way through the game. The sequel, though, also throws up complications like biomes, where environmental dangers and opportunities must be dodged and seized. Read more

Source: Nature Human Behaviour

### Microdelays disrupt online learning
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC

Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 08 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02598-y Analysing data from more than 80,000 students and aspiring coders, Mazar et al. show that microdelays, brief loading lags caused by slow internet connections, can impair online learning.

### Recreational screen use and internalizing problems from preadolescence to young adulthood in a longitudinal twin cohort with co-twin comparison
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02607-0
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 08 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02607-0 Using longitudinal twin data, this study finds that associations between youth recreational screen use and internalizing problems are substantially reduced within twin pairs, suggesting shared familial contributions to the observed association.

### Simulated responses from large language models are weak proxies for human economic preferences across 74 countries
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02611-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02611-4 Simulating a global survey of economic preferences across 74 countries, this study shows that large language models reproduce some demographic patterns in human preferences but fail to capture cultural differences, especially in lower-income countries.

### Both selfish and prosocial actors distort their impact on others, but selfish-serving spin seems to spread
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02584-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02584-4 From consumers buying fair-trade products to policy makers funding science, people often do not know exactly how their actions will affect others. We show that selfish and prosocial actors alike may distort their impact in self-serving ways, but selfish-serving distortions catch on more readily than prosocial-serving distortions and increase followers’ selfishness.

### Recognizing Indigenous peoples
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02619-w
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02619-w Settler states increasingly offer Indigenous peoples symbolic recognition, such as land acknowledgements, instead of political power or resources, yet the effects of these gestures are untested. Peyton and McNamee’s experiments in the USA and Australia find that land acknowledgements raise awareness of Indigenous peoples but reduce interest in learning more.

## academic research / 学术研究

### Ancient Greek votive epigraphy: Ancient religion between individual choice and cultural trends
Source: PLOS ONE Biology
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0361143
Source meta: journal · primary · R4 · P4 · signal

by Moshe Blidstein Ancient votive inscriptions, numbering in the tens of thousands across the Mediterranean, offer direct evidence for religious practice but have rarely been studied comprehensively across time and space due to their sheer volume. This paper presents a “distant reading” analysis of approximately 11,000 Greek dedicatory inscriptions (sixth century BCE – seventh century CE) drawn primarily from the GIST/I.PHI digital corpus. By tagging inscriptions for key features (votive/dedicatory terminology, deities, motives, gender) and analyzing their distribution chronologically and geographically while controlling for the “epigraphic habit,” this study identifies large-scale trends. The results reveal a significant relative increase in the use of explicit votive terminology (forms of euche / euchomai ) over time, particularly during the Roman Imperial period and Late Antiquity. This practice exhibits strong regional variation, being markedly more prevalent in Asia Minor and the East than in mainland Greece before the fourth century CE.

### Eastern origin and three-millennia persistence of a founding grapevine lineage in Iberian viticulture
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01090-0?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Pérez-Jordà et al. use archaeogenomics to show that eastern domesticated grapevines laid the foundations for Iberian viticulture 3,000 years ago. Genetic relatedness indicates these introductions gave rise to extant Iberian cultivars through a few sexual generations, revealing clonal propagation of the cultivar Heben from the 10th century to the present.

### Ketamine attenuates habenula activity in response to aversive outcomes during Pavlovian learning
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01095-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Translating a preclinical model to humans using 7-tesla MRI, Pulcu et al. show that ketamine reduces habenula activity for the expectation of aversive outcomes during Pavlovian learning. This provides direct translational evidence for how ketamine modulates the neural circuitry implicated in depression, relevant to its rapid antidepressant effects.

### Cambrian fossils illuminate the ambulacrarian common ancestor
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01104-8?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

He et al. describe Yujingia, a new early Cambrian ambulacrarian with differentiated appendages that include pinnate arms with filter-feeding grooves. The morphology and phylogenetic position of Yujingia suggest that modern ambulacrarians, including echinoderms and hemichordates, evolved from a tentaculate, semi-sessile suspension-feeding ancestor.

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

### Thirst reweights temperature preference through dual dopaminergic and endocrine modulation in Drosophila
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01114-0?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Chiang et al. show that thirst shifts Drosophila toward cooler temperatures by bidirectionally reweighting cold and hot responses in the mushroom body. Dopamine- and 20E-mediated pathways differentially tune these responses, which converge on a common avoidance-promoting output.

### Spatial proteomics reveals carboxysome adaptation and mitochondrial proteome in Paulinella chromatophora
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01113-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Using LOPIT-DC-based spatial proteomics, Cadena et al. map the sub-organellar protein landscape of Paulinella chromatophora, a cercozoan with evolutionary-early-stage photosynthetic organelles. They find nucleus-encoded carboxysome-associated proteins potentially forming a novel O2-scavenging system that may enhance carbon fixation efficiency. The mitochondrial sub-proteome reveals conserved membrane organization alongside divergent, lineage-specific proteins.

### Three ways that international financial architecture reform matters for biodiversity
Source: Nature Ecology & Evolution
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41559-026-03194-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Ecology & Evolution, Published online: 09 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41559-026-03194-z Achieving Global Biodiversity Framework targets will require addressing not only the direct drivers of biodiversity loss but also the global financial rules that shape them. We argue that the current international financial architecture reinforces extractive development pathways, constrains investment in ecological transition, and limits ambitious environmental policy, which makes financial system reform an essential component of the transformative change needed for biodiversity protection.

Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Ecology & Evolution, Published online: 09 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41559-026-03212-0 China’s new National Parks Law marks a clear shift from fragmented protected-area governance to a unified, legally binding conservation framework that operates at a national scale. Its success will depend on how well scientific evidence is integrated into its implementation; progress is needed in climate-adaptive planning, monitoring, and community-based governance to ensure that its legal ambition delivers lasting ecological benefits.

### [Review] Addressing threats to future global health—what is the right timeframe?
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00880-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Our decisions today can support or threaten future human health. Insofar as we protect the planet or invest in fruitful research on pandemic countermeasures, we promote future wellbeing and health, sometimes at the present generation's expense. As we warm the planet, accelerate antibiotic resistance, or consume finite natural resources, we might serve present-day wellbeing or health at the expense of future generations. To decide which such trade-offs are legitimate or advisable, we need to know how to balance the health interests of future generations against each other and against our own.

### [The Lancet Commissions] The Lancet Commission on 21st century global threats to health
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01714-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This report from the Lancet Commission on 21st century global threats to health lays out a foresight-driven, health-focused approach to identifying and prioritising major threats to global health over the remainder of the century. We argue that recent global shocks (including COVID-19, Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease, major geopolitical conflict, accelerating climate impacts, rapid shifts in development assistance, and the emergence of powerful artificial intelligence [AI] systems) show how quickly assumptions about stability can be overturned—and why governments and multilateral institutions must counter short-termism and localism with systematic, repeatable risk assessment and preparedness planning.

### A Boson-informed neural network for Ground States of Many-Body Schr\"{o}dinger equations
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-15 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18875
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.18875v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We propose a symmetry-preserving Boson-informed neural network (BINN) for the variational Monte Carlo computation of interacting-boson ground states in continuous space. The framework explicitly separates the two dominant physical scales of the system. At the microscopic interaction scale, a cell Jastrow factor absorbs the leading near-contact stiffness, while at the macroscopic scale, a potential-aware envelope encodes the external confinement, leaving the neural network to capture a smoother many-body residual. Across both two- and three-dimensional tests, the BINN approaches the Hartree and Gross-Pitaevskii limits with an empirical $\mathcal{O}(N^{-1})$ finite-size trend in both energy and density. The energies agree with diffusion Monte Carlo to within $0.06\%$ for the particle number tested up to $350$ in the three-dimensional shrinking-range case. Applications are made under various trapping scenarios, justifying robust performance of BINN.

### Physics-Guided Multi-Fidelity Surrogate Modeling for Natural Supercavity Geometry of an Axisymmetric Conical Cavitator
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-15 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18886
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.18886v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Natural supercavitation behind conical cavitators generates a vapor-filled envelope that, when sustained over the full body of a high-speed underwater vehicles, reduces hydrodynamic skin-friction drag and enables performance levels unattainable in fully wetted flow. Accurate prediction of maximum cavity length and maximum cavity radius and local cavity profile as functions of cavitation number and cone half-angle is essential for both the static design and the real-time shape management of super-cavitating torpedo cavitators. Classical analytical models provide computationally inexpensive predictions but exhibit systematic disagreements in the practically important regime of moderate cavitation number and large angle, where slender-body assumptions begin to break down. Existing data-driven machine learning approaches offer higher accuracy but are constrained by the prohibitive cost of multiphase computational fluid dynamics (CFD) sweeps over the full design space.

### Supercell-enabled inverse design of boundary-state dispersion for group-velocity control in pseudomagnetic phononic crystals
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-15 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18908
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.18908v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Topological boundary states offer a platform for guided-wave propagation within bulk bandgaps. Their propagation characteristics depend not only on the spectral extent of the states but also on their dispersion within the operating bandwidth. A nearly linear dispersion provides more uniform group velocity across frequency, whereas a reduced slope enables slow-wave propagation. Despite advances in inverse design of prescribed dispersion relations and topological dispersion tuning, application-oriented characteristics of boundary-state dispersion have rarely been direct inverse-design objectives. Here, we develop a supercell-enabled, surrogate-assisted framework for the inverse design of boundary-state dispersion in a phononic crystal with pseudomagnetic fields. Area-preserving anisotropic deformation shifts the local Dirac points, and independent variation of 21 rows within the supercell provides distributed control over the boundary-state branch.

### A Multi-frame Analysis of Flow Reversal in Tidal Bore
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-15 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18913
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.18913v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: A tidal bore is a nonlinear step like surge propagating on the top of a river counter-current. The scientific literature reports that the passage of the tidal front may induce local flow reversals and, in some cases, a global reversal of the river current. Here, combining Particle Image Velocimetry (PIV) measurements and 2D numerical simulations based on a Weakly Compressible Smoothed Particle Hydrodynamics (WCSPH) method, we show that the flow classification of tidal bores must explicitly take into account the frame of reference. When the river flow does not reverse after the bore passing, this is a consequence of the perspective imposed by the background current (itself resulting from the previous tide and the natural river discharge toward the sea). Depending on the relative magnitudes of the river current and the velocity field induced by the bore, one may observe a global reversal, a local reversal (or both), or no reversal at all.

### Popularity Trajectories and Mixing Patterns of Urban Venues in Two European Cities
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-15 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18917
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.18917v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Urban venues such as restaurants, shops, bars and parks host the daily life of a city, and their collective rise and fall signals how vibrant a neighbourhood is. The usual assumption is that a venue's fortunes follow its neighbourhood's, so that how wealthy an area is and how many people live there will decide a venue's performance. Yet whether there is a common performance trajectory shared by venues over time is underexplored. In this study, we use 5.5 years of Foursquare data for about 10^4 venues in London and Barcelona. We found six distinguishable shapes recur in both cities, that are (1) Fall, (2) Rise, (3) Rise & Fall, (4) Steady, (5) Ephemeral (an early hit that loses popularity fast) and (6) Underdogs (a slow starter that gains popularity late). However, no recovery shape appears, that is neither city yields a U-shaped fall-and-rise group. Additionally, we found that deprivation and population density are not indicative which shape a venue follows.

### TorchSpin: Differentiable Electron Paramagnetic Resonance Simulation in PyTorch
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-15 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18924
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.18924v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Interpreting electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) spectra commonly requires repeated forward simulation and optimization of spin Hamiltonian parameters. Established software provides mature simulation and fitting methods, but these calculations are not generally available as differentiable components within machine-learning workflows. We present TorchSpin, an open-source PyTorch framework that ports the organization and numerical conventions of EasySpin into a tensor-based computational environment. Representative calculations spanning continuous-wave EPR, ENDOR, pulse EPR, spin dynamics, trajectory simulation, and magnetometry closely reproduced corresponding EasySpin results. In matched benchmarks, TorchSpin CPU execution was faster than EasySpin for six of nine workloads although performance depended strongly on the calculation. We therefore focused on the capabilities enabled by automatic differentiation in PyTorch.

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Brown dwarfs are the black (or brown?) sheep of astronomy. They’re too big to be considered a traditional gas planet, but they are too small to have started nuclear fusion and made the full transition to star-hood. They also change dramatically over time, cooling, shrinking, and spinning faster as they age. Or at least we think they do - our models of the atmospheres of these celestial objects aren’t the best, according to a new paper in The Astrophysical Journal Letters by a team led by Zafar Rustamkulov at Caltech and NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory.

## computing cultures / 计算文化

### Importing a frontend Javascript library without a build system
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2024/11/18/how-to-import-a-javascript-library/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

I like writing Javascript without a build system and for the millionth time yesterday I ran into a problem where I needed to figure out how to import a Javascript library in my code without using a build system, and it took FOREVER to figure out how to import it because the library’s setup instructions assume that you’re using a build system. Luckily at this point I’ve mostly learned how to navigate this situation and either successfully use the library or decide it’s too difficult and switch to a different library, so here’s the guide I wish I had to importing Javascript libraries years ago. I’m only going to talk about using Javacript libraries on the frontend, and only about how to use them in a no-build-system setup.

### Why pipes sometimes get "stuck": buffering
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2024/11/29/why-pipes-get-stuck-buffering/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Here’s a niche terminal problem that has bothered me for years but that I never really understood until a few weeks ago. Let’s say you’re running this command to watch for some specific output in a log file: tail -f /some/log/file | grep thing1 | grep thing2 If log lines are being added to the file relatively slowly, the result I’d see is… nothing! It doesn’t matter if there were matches in the log file or not, there just wouldn’t be any output. I internalized this as “uh, I guess pipes just get stuck sometimes and don’t show me the output, that’s weird”, and I’d handle it by just running grep thing1 /some/log/file | grep thing2 instead, which would work. So as I’ve been doing a terminal deep dive over the last few months I was really excited to finally learn exactly why this happens. why this happens: buffering The reason why “pipes get stuck” sometimes is that it’s VERY common for programs to buffer their output before writing it to a pipe or file.

### "Rules" that terminal programs follow
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2024/11/26/terminal-rules/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Recently I’ve been thinking about how everything that happens in the terminal is some combination of: Your operating system ’s job Your shell ’s job Your terminal emulator ’s job The job of whatever program you happen to be running (like top or vim or cat ) The first three (your operating system, shell, and terminal emulator) are all kind of known quantities – if you’re using bash in GNOME Terminal on Linux, you can more or less reason about how how all of those things interact, and some of their behaviour is standardized by POSIX. But the fourth one (“whatever program you happen to be running”) feels like it could do ANYTHING. How are you supposed to know how a program is going to behave?

### What's involved in getting a "modern" terminal setup?
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2025/01/11/getting-a-modern-terminal-setup/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Hello! Recently I ran a terminal survey and I asked people what frustrated them. One person commented: There are so many pieces to having a modern terminal experience. I wish it all came out of the box. My immediate reaction was “oh, getting a modern terminal experience isn’t that hard, you just need to….”, but the more I thought about it, the longer the “you just need to…” list got, and I kept thinking about more and more caveats. So I thought I would write down some notes about what it means to me personally to have a “modern” terminal experience and what I think can make it hard for people to get there. what is a “modern terminal experience”? Here are a few things that are important to me, with which part of the system is responsible for them: multiline support for copy and paste : if you paste 3 commands in your shell, it should not immediately run them all! That’s scary!

### Some terminal frustrations
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2025/02/05/some-terminal-frustrations/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A few weeks ago I ran a terminal survey (you can read the results here ) and at the end I asked: What’s the most frustrating thing about using the terminal for you? 1600 people answered, and I decided to spend a few days categorizing all the responses. Along the way I learned that classifying qualitative data is not easy but I gave it my best shot. I ended up building a custom tool to make it faster to categorize everything. As with all of my surveys the methodology isn’t particularly scientific. I just posted the survey to Mastodon and Twitter, ran it for a couple of days, and got answers from whoever happened to see it and felt like responding. Here are the top categories of frustrations! I think it’s worth keeping in mind while reading these comments that 40% of people answering this survey have been using the terminal for 21+ years 95% of people answering the survey have been using the terminal for at least 4 years These comments aren’t coming from total beginners. Here are the categories of frustrations!

### How to add a directory to your PATH
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2025/02/13/how-to-add-a-directory-to-your-path/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

I was talking to a friend about how to add a directory to your PATH today. It’s something that feels “obvious” to me since I’ve been using the terminal for a long time, but when I searched for instructions for how to do it, I actually couldn’t find something that explained all of the steps – a lot of them just said “add this to ~/.bashrc ”, but what if you’re not using bash? What if your bash config is actually in a different file? And how are you supposed to figure out which directory to add anyway? So I wanted to try to write down some more complete directions and mention some of the gotchas I’ve run into over the years. Here’s a table of contents: step 1: what shell are you using? step 2: find your shell’s config file a note on bash’s config file step 3: figure out which directory to add step 3.

### The Orchestrator's Tax
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-15 22:50 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/articles/orchestrator-tax.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Subagents get justified by time saved and parallel execution, but Rahul Garg explains that's not what matters most. Every token in the orchestrator's context is competing for its attention, and the real value of a subagent is what it keeps out of that context. Subagents should be treated as a tool for protecting the orchestrator's working memory, offloading reasoning it doesn't need to hold onto. Doing this well means giving the orchestrator explicit ground rules for when and how to delegate. more…
